官方论文页面www.computer.org · 2026-10-24 12:34 CST · 论文身份与出版状态证据 DOIdoi.org · 2026-10-24 12:34 CST · 规范化论文身份

摘要（可展开，供朗读/NotebookLM）

论文题名

Wisdom is Knowledge Combined With Intellect: Knowledge-Embedded Hypergraph-of-Thought Reasoning for Visual Abductive Ratiocination

期刊：IEEE Transactions on Pattern Analysis and Machine Intelligence · DOI：10.1109/tpami.2026.3697912 · 语言：en · 摘要来源：出版方摘要

For a still image, humans can abductively ratiocinate various probable inferences and hypotheses beyond the given image from visual clues scattered throughout a pictorial scene. However, achieving such a task, i.e., visually abductive ratiocination, is challenging for models because of the limitations of observations, knowledge, and their synthesis process. Therefore, we propose a novel multimodal model: knowledge-embedded hypergraph-of-thought reasoning. First, it represents a physical scene as a semantic graph and a hypergraph to capture the underlying intra- and inter- visual -linguistic relationships, thereby overcoming the limitations of observations derived from the spatial domain. Then, a multimodal knowledge hypergraph is constructed to discriminate and associate scene-related multimodal multigranular concepts with a hierarchical structure, leveraging web resources. This approach overcomes the limitations of lacking world knowledge. Finally, a multimodal hypergraph-of-thought reasoning paradigm is devised based on the semantic and knowledge hypergraphs to model the higher-order relationships and interaction mechanisms between the graphs for conducting multihop reasoning, thus overcoming the limitations of semantics and knowledge being separate and independent. Our method reflects the philosophy of “wisdom is knowledge combined with intellect” in the sense that reasoning models including multimodal knowledge and semantic representations are more robust than those that do not synthesize them. The experimental results show that our model achieves significantly greater performance than state-of-the-art methods do.

SGFormer: Simplifying and Scaling Graph Transformers With Single-Layer Attention and Approximation-Free Linear Complexity

Qitian Wu; Kai Yang; Hengrui Zhang; David Wipf; Junchi Yan

卷期
V48 · I10
门控
NEW_ARTICLE
DOI
10.1109/tpami.2026.3697944
证据层级
METADATA

官方论文页面www.computer.org · 2026-10-24 12:34 CST · 论文身份与出版状态证据 DOIdoi.org · 2026-10-24 12:34 CST · 规范化论文身份

摘要（可展开，供朗读/NotebookLM）

论文题名

SGFormer: Simplifying and Scaling Graph Transformers With Single-Layer Attention and Approximation-Free Linear Complexity

期刊：IEEE Transactions on Pattern Analysis and Machine Intelligence · DOI：10.1109/tpami.2026.3697944 · 语言：en · 摘要来源：出版方摘要

Learning representations on large graphs is a fundamental challenge due to complex inter-dependencies. While Transformers excel on small graphs via global attention, existing architectures often mirror large language models by stacking deep attention layers. This design philosophy restricts the scalability of Transformers on large graphs, as the unique inter-dependency nature makes it non-trivial to losslessly partition a graph for modern accelerators. We provide a theoretical reassessment of whether deep attention is a necessity. Our analysis shows that for a generic hybrid propagation layer that combines global attention and graph-based propagation, multi-layer models can be reduced to one-layer counterparts without sacrificing representation capacity. Guided by these insights, we propose Simplified Single-Layer Graph Transformer (SGFormer), which utilizes single-layer global attention with approximation-free linear complexity. Unlike scalable Transformers that rely on stochastic approximations or restricted receptive fields, SGFormer scales exactly linearly w.r.t. graph sizes and requires none of any approximation for accommodating all-pair interactions. Empirically, it yields orders-of-magnitude inference acceleration over state-of-the-art Transformers on medium-sized graphs and scales smoothly to the web-scale ogbn-papers100 M dataset (0.1B nodes) on a single GPU with 24 GB memory. Our results suggest that principled simplification is a highly effective path for powerful, scalable foundation models for large-graph learning.

GVHMR: Gravity-View Coordinates for Global Human Motion Recovery From Monocular Videos

Zehong Shen; Jiawei Wang; Huaijin Pi; Yan Xia; Zhi Cen; Zechen Hu; Hujun Bao; Ruizhen Hu; Xiaowei Zhou; Sida Peng

卷期
V48 · I10
门控

10.1109/tpami.2026.3699758
证据层级
METADATA

官方论文页面www.computer.org · 2026-10-24 12:34 CST · 论文身份与出版状态证据 DOIdoi.org · 2026-10-24 12:34 CST · 规范化论文身份

摘要（可展开，供朗读/NotebookLM）

论文题名

GVHMR: Gravity-View Coordinates for Global Human Motion Recovery From Monocular Videos

期刊：IEEE Transactions on Pattern Analysis and Machine Intelligence · DOI：10.1109/tpami.2026.3699758 · 语言：en · 摘要来源：出版方摘要

We present a novel method for recovering world-grounded human motion from monocular video. The main challenge lies in the ambiguity of defining the world coordinate system, which varies between sequences. Previous approaches attempt to alleviate this issue by predicting relative motion in an auto-regressive manner, but are prone to accumulating errors. Instead, we propose estimating human poses in a novel Gravity-View (GV) coordinate system, which is defined by the world gravity and the camera view direction. The proposed GV system is naturally gravity-aligned and uniquely defined for each video frame, largely reducing the ambiguity of learning image-pose mapping. The estimated poses can be transformed back to the world coordinate system using camera rotations, forming a global motion sequence. Additionally, the per-frame estimation avoids error accumulation in the auto-regressive methods. Experiments on in-the-wild benchmarks demonstrate that our method recovers more realistic motion in both the camera space and world-grounded settings, outperforming state-of-the-art methods in both accuracy and speed. We further introduce a Stationary Label Predictor (SLP) to estimate the motion stationary states of hands and feet. By producing sharper decision boundaries, the module mitigates unnatural artifacts such as foot-sliding and shows improved performance under fast-motion inputs.

UniMM: A Unified Mixture Model Framework for Multi-Agent Simulation

Longzhong Lin; Xuewu Lin; Kechun Xu; Haojian Lu; Lichao Huang; Rong Xiong; Yue Wang

卷期

官方论文页面www.computer.org · 2026-10-24 12:34 CST · 论文身份与出版状态证据 DOIdoi.org · 2026-10-24 12:34 CST · 规范化论文身份

摘要（可展开，供朗读/NotebookLM）

论文题名

UniMM: A Unified Mixture Model Framework for Multi-Agent Simulation

期刊：IEEE Transactions on Pattern Analysis and Machine Intelligence · DOI：10.1109/tpami.2026.3700402 · 语言：en · 摘要来源：出版方摘要

Simulation plays a crucial role in assessing autonomous driving systems, where the generation of realistic multi-agent behaviors is a key aspect. In multi-agent simulation, the primary challenges include behavioral multimodality and closed-loop distributional shifts. In this study, we formulate a unified mixture model (UniMM) framework for generating multimodal agent behaviors, which can cover the mainstream methods including regression-based mixture models and discrete NTP models. Furthermore, we introduce a closed-loop sample generation approach tailored for mixture models to mitigate distributional shifts. Within the UniMM framework, we recognize critical configurations from both the model and data perspectives. We conduct a systematic examination of various model configurations, and comprehensively characterize their effects. Moreover, our investigation into the data configuration highlights the pivotal role of closed-loop samples in achieving realistic simulations. To extend the benefits of closed-loop samples across a broader range of mixture models, we further introduce a temporal disentanglement-and-alignment mechanism to address the shortcut learning and off-policy learning issues. Leveraging insights from our exploration, the distinct variants proposed within the UniMM framework, including discrete, anchor-free, and anchor-based models, all achieve state-of-the-art performance on the WOSAC benchmark.

Optimizing Trust and Safety Regions for Text-to-Image Generation in High-Dimensional Manifold Spaces

Xiang Yang; Xiaohui Li; Yuke Wang; Ninghao Liu

卷期
V48 · I10
门控
NEW_ARTICLE
DOI
10.1109/tpami.2026.3699945
证据层级
METADATA

官方论文页面www.computer.org · 2026-10-24 12:34 CST · 论文身份与出版状态证据 DOIdoi.org · 2026-10-24 12:34 CST · 规范化论文身份

摘要（可展开，供朗读/NotebookLM）

论文题名

Optimizing Trust and Safety Regions for Text-to-Image Generation in High-Dimensional Manifold Spaces

期刊：IEEE Transactions on Pattern Analysis and Machine Intelligence · DOI：10.1109/tpami.2026.3699945 · 语言：en · 摘要来源：出版方摘要

Diffusion-based text-to-image (T2I) models such as Stable Diffusion (SD) and DALL ⋅ E 2 enable versatile image generation but raise significant safety concerns due to their ability to produce harmful or not-safe-for-work (NSFW) content (e.g., nudity). Existing safety strategies, including prompt filtering and machine unlearning, remain limited, as they are vulnerable to biased data, model openness, and adversarial prompt attacks. Achieving safe alignment during reinforcement learning (RL) fine-tuning is thus essential, yet faces two significant challenges: alignment fragility, where models easily lose control after optimization, and the safety–quality paradox, where improving safety often degrades visual quality. To address these issues, we propose S-TRPO, a Safety-constrained Trust-Region Policy Optimization framework that enables safe and reliable alignment of diffusion models (DMs) within the manifold policy space. S-TRPO introduces a dynamic safety-control mechanism that combines danger-region perception with trust-region constraints to maintain both safety and generation fidelity. Specifically, a KL-based safety region and a static risk model jointly evaluate harmful prompt risk and restrict unsafe deviations in policy updates. Furthermore, a Lagrangian dual-control scheme balances safety constraints with image-quality optimization. Extensive experiments on real-world adversarial benchmarks demonstrate that, under white-box UnlearnDiffAtk evaluation, S-TRPO with full malicious fine-tuning reduces the attack success rate by 51.7% relative to DPOK, while maintaining comparable image-text alignment quality. These results highlight the effectiveness of S-TRPO in mitigating risky behaviors and enhancing the reliability of T2I diffusion systems.

Kaleab A. Kinfu; Rene Vidal

卷期
V48 · I10
门控
NEW_ARTICLE

10.1109/tpami.2026.3701408
证据层级
METADATA

官方论文页面www.computer.org · 2026-10-24 12:34 CST · 论文身份与出版状态证据 DOIdoi.org · 2026-10-24 12:34 CST · 规范化论文身份

摘要（可展开，供朗读/NotebookLM）

论文题名

Transformers With Joint Tokens and Local-Global Attention for Efficient Human Pose Estimation

期刊：IEEE Transactions on Pattern Analysis and Machine Intelligence · DOI：10.1109/tpami.2026.3701408 · 语言：en · 摘要来源：出版方摘要

Convolutional Neural Networks (CNNs) and Vision Transformers (ViTs) have driven significant progress in 2D human pose estimation. However, achieving a good balance between accuracy, efficiency, and robustness remains challenging. For instance, CNNs are computationally efficient but struggle with long-range dependencies, while ViTs excel in capturing such dependencies but suffer from quadratic computational complexity with respect to the number of patch tokens. This paper proposes two ViT-based models for accurate, efficient, and robust 2D pose estimation. The first, EViTPose, improves computational efficiency with minimal accuracy loss by utilizing learnable joint tokens to select and process a subset of patches most relevant to the body joints, thereby enabling control over the accuracy efficiency trade-off by varying the number of processed patches. The second, UniTransPose, while not providing the same direct control over this trade-off, efficiently handles multiple scales by combining (1) an efficient multi-scale transformer encoder using both local and global attention and (2) an efficient sub-pixel CNN decoder for improved speed and accuracy. Furthermore, by incorporating joint annotation schemas from different benchmarks into a unified skeletal representation, we train robust models that learn from multiple datasets simultaneously and perform well across a range of scenarios, including variations in pose, lighting, and occlusion. Experiments on six benchmarks demonstrate that the proposed methods substantially outperform state-ofthe-art methods while improving computational efficiency. EViTPose significantly reduces computational cost (reduces GFLOPs by 30%44%) with minimal drop in accuracy (0% to 3.5%), and UniTransPose achieves accuracy improvements ranging from 0.9% to 43.8% across these benchmarks.

Weilun Feng; Chuanguang Yang; Haotong Qin; Yuqi Li; Xiangqi Li; Zhulin An; Libo Huang; Boyu Diao; Fuzhen Zhuang; Michele Magno; Yongjun Xu; Yingli Tian; Tingwen Huang

METADATA

官方论文页面www.computer.org · 2026-10-24 12:34 CST · 论文身份与出版状态证据 DOIdoi.org · 2026-10-24 12:34 CST · 规范化论文身份

摘要（可展开，供朗读/NotebookLM）

论文题名

MPQ-DMv2: Flexible Residual Mixed Precision Quantization for Low-Bit Diffusion Models With Temporal Distillation

期刊：IEEE Transactions on Pattern Analysis and Machine Intelligence · DOI：10.1109/tpami.2026.3700583 · 语言：en · 摘要来源：出版方摘要

Diffusion models have demonstrated remarkable performance on vision generation tasks. However, the high computational complexity hinders its wide application on edge devices. Quantization has emerged as a promising technique for inference acceleration and memory reduction. However, existing quantization methods do not generalize well under extremely low-bit (2-4 b) quantization. Directly applying these methods will cause severe performance degradation. We identify that the existing quantization framework suffers from the outlier-unfriendly quantizer design, suboptimal initialization, and optimization strategy. We present MPQ-DMv2, an improved Mixed Precision Quantization framework for extremely low-bit Diffusion Models. For the quantization perspective, the imbalanced distribution caused by salient outliers is quantization-unfriendly for uniform quantizer. We propose Flexible Z-Order Residual Mixed Quantization that utilizes an efficient binary residual branch for flexible quant steps to handle salient error. For the optimization framework, we theoretically analyzed the convergence and optimality of the LoRA module and propose Object-Oriented Low-Rank Initialization to use prior quantization error for informative initialization. We then propose Memory-based Temporal Relation Distillation to construct an online time-aware pixel queue for long-term denoising temporal information distillation, which ensures the overall temporal consistency between quantized and full-precision model. Comprehensive experiments on various generation tasks show that our MPQ-DMv2 surpasses current SOTA methods by a great margin on different architectures, especially under extremely low-bit widths.

Selecting and Distilling Cross-Label Models

Su Lu; Han-Jia Ye; De-Chuan Zhan

卷期
V48 · I10
门控
NEW_ARTICLE
DOI
10.1109/tpami.2026.3700851
证据层级
METADATA

官方论文页面www.computer.org · 2026-10-24 12:34 CST · 论文身份与出版状态证据 DOIdoi.org · 2026-10-24 12:34 CST · 规范化论文身份

摘要（可展开，供朗读/NotebookLM）

论文题名

Selecting and Distilling Cross-Label Models

期刊：IEEE Transactions on Pattern Analysis and Machine Intelligence · DOI：10.1109/tpami.2026.3700851 · 语言：en · 摘要来源：出版方摘要

The practice of knowledge distillation, wherein pre-trained models — termed “teachers” — serve to enhance the performance of subsequent “student” models, has seen a surge in popularity. With a lot of pre-trained models available, there is a pressing need to investigate effective mechanisms for utilizing these resources optimally. This paper tackles two main challenges inherent in developing such mechanisms. The initial challenge lies in the effective selection of the most advantageous models from a vast pool, given that exhaustively testing each pre-trained model is impractical. The second challenge stems from the diversity of pre-trained models, as it is common for a pre-trained model to have a different label space than that of the current task. This necessitates the development of a universal model reuse approach capable of integrating any pre-trained model, regardless of its unknown label set. To address these issues, we introduce a dual-phase framework named “Selective Cross-Label Distillation.” The first phase, termed model assessment, evaluates the semantic similarity between a potential pre-trained model and the target model through optimal transport. By determining the transportation cost, we pinpoint candidate models with lower costs to serve as our source models. The second phase, knowledge reuse, concentrates on minimizing the transportation cost between the chosen source models and the target model. This dual-phase approach enables efficient model selection and bridges the semantic gap between pre-trained models and the target task, leading to enhanced performance. The effectiveness of our framework in model selection and knowledge reuse is validated by experimental results.

Federated Learning via Variational Bayesian Inference: Personalization, Sparsity and Clustering

Xu Zhang; Wenpeng Li; Yunfeng Shao; Yonglin Liu; Kaiwen Zhou; Yinchuan Li

卷期
V48 · I10
门控
METADATA_CHANGED
DOI
10.1109/tpami.2026.3699626
证据层级
METADATA

官方论文页面www.computer.org · 2026-10-24 12:34 CST · 论文身份与出版状态证据 DOIdoi.org · 2026-10-24 12:34 CST · 规范化论文身份

摘要（可展开，供朗读/NotebookLM）

论文题名

Federated Learning via Variational Bayesian Inference: Personalization, Sparsity and Clustering

期刊：IEEE Transactions on Pattern Analysis and Machine Intelligence · DOI：10.1109/tpami.2026.3699626 · 语言：en · 摘要来源：出版方摘要

Federated learning (FL) is a promising framework that models distributed machine learning while protecting the privacy of clients. However, FL suffers performance degradation from heterogeneous and limited data. To alleviate the degradation, we present a novel personalized Bayesian FL approach named pFedBayes. By using the trained global distribution from the server as the prior distribution of each client, each client adjusts its own distribution by minimizing the sum of the reconstruction error over its personalized data and the KL divergence with the downloaded global distribution. Then, we propose a sparse personalized Bayesian FL approach named sFedBayes to enhance the inference efficiency. To overcome the extreme heterogeneity in non-i.i.d. data, we propose a clustered Bayesian FL model named cFedbayes by learning different prior distributions for different clients. Theoretical analysis gives the generalization error bound of three approaches and shows that the generalization error rates of the proposed approaches achieve minimax optimality up to a logarithmic factor. Moreover, cFedBayes achieves a cluster-level generalization error bound, rather than a single uniform bound in pFedBayes. Numerous experiments demonstrate that the proposed approaches have better performance than other advanced personalized methods on private models in the presence of heterogeneous and limited data.

Person-in-WiFi 3D: Unified Model for 3D WiFi Perception

Bo Qian; Xing Wei; Kangwei Yan; Han Ding; Jinsong Han; Fei Wang

卷期
V48 · I10
门控
METADATA_CHANGED
DOI
10.1109/tpami.2026.3701032
证据层级
METADATA

官方论文页面www.computer.org · 2026-10-24 12:34 CST · 论文身份与出版状态证据 DOIdoi.org · 2026-10-24 12:34 CST · 规范化论文身份

摘要（可展开，供朗读/NotebookLM）

论文题名

Person-in-WiFi 3D: Unified Model for 3D WiFi Perception

期刊：IEEE Transactions on Pattern Analysis and Machine Intelligence · DOI：10.1109/tpami.2026.3701032 · 语言：en · 摘要来源：出版方摘要

WiFi sensing offers a privacy-preserving and occlusion-robust alternative to camera-based human perception, making it attractive for applications such as smart homes, elderly care, and virtual reality. Although recent studies have achieved remarkable progress in WiFi-based single-person 2D/3D pose estimation, multi-person 2D pose estimation, and single-person mesh reconstruction, multi-person 3D human perception remains largely unexplored. In this paper, we present Person-in-WiFi 3D, the first WiFi sensing system capable of end-to-end multi-person 3D pose estimation and mesh reconstruction. Person-in-WiFi 3D advances prior work in two aspects. First, it employs multiple WiFi transmitters and receivers to capture richer 3D spatial reflections from multiple individuals, enabling robust perception in complex multi-person scenarios. Second, it introduces a DETR-inspired architecture with Hungarian matching and a coarse-to-fine hierarchical refinement strategy that progressively exploits global, instance-level, and keypoint-level features for accurate human localization and reconstruction. These designs enable efficient, scalable, and high-fidelity 3D human perception.To support research in this area, we construct Wiception3D, the first large-scale dataset for WiFi-based multi-person 3D human perception. The dataset contains over 97,000 frames collected from seven participants across diverse multi-person activities in a 4 m × 3.5 m environment, with annotations for both 3D pose estimation and 3D mesh reconstruction. Experimental results show that Person-in-WiFi 3D achieves a mean 3D keypoint localization error of 93 mm and a mesh reconstruction error of 41 mm, delivering performance comparable to camera- and millimeter-wave radar-based systems while retaining the inherent advantages of WiFi sensing.

A Theoretical Characterization of the Good Properties of Extremely Randomized Trees for Random Forest-Distance Computation

Manuele Bicego; Ferdinando Cicalese

卷期
V48 · I10
门控
NEW_ARTICLE
DOI
10.1109/tpami.2026.3702239
证据层级
METADATA

官方论文页面www.computer.org · 2026-10-24 12:34 CST · 论文身份与出版状态证据 DOIdoi.org · 2026-10-24 12:34 CST · 规范化论文身份

摘要（可展开，供朗读/NotebookLM）

论文题名

A Theoretical Characterization of the Good Properties of Extremely Randomized Trees for Random Forest-Distance Computation

期刊：IEEE Transactions on Pattern Analysis and Machine Intelligence · DOI：10.1109/tpami.2026.3702239 · 语言：en · 摘要来源：出版方摘要

Ensembles of Extremely Randomized Trees (ERT), initially introduced for supervised scenarios, have been shown to represent surprisingly effective models also in unsupervised contexts, for example, for the detection of anomalies (i.e., Isolation Forests) or for defining Random Forest-distances. In this paper, we focus on this last field, providing a theoretical characterization to support the excellent empirical behaviors shown by ERTs in the definition of RF-distances. We start by assuming the existence, in a given domain, of a proper representation, i.e., a vectorial representation fulfilling the Compactness Hypothesis given by Arkadev and Braverman in 1967. Then, given the “true” distance between a pair of objects, we show that it is possible to derive a bound on the approximation guaranteed by an ERT-based RF-distance with respect to such true measure. In words, we show that it is possible to compute a constant c such that, if a pair of objects are ϵ -close in the true distance, then, with high probability, they are (c⋅ϵ) -close also in the ERT-based RF-distance.

SJD++: Improved Speculative Jacobi Decoding for Training-Free Acceleration of Discrete Auto-Regressive Text-to-Image Generation

Yao Teng; Zhihuan Jiang; Han Shi; Xian Liu; Xuefei Ning; Guohao Dai; Yu Wang; Zhenguo Li; Xihui Liu

卷期
V48 · I10
门控
NEW_ARTICLE
DOI
10.1109/tpami.2026.3700227
证据层级
METADATA

官方论文页面www.computer.org · 2026-10-24 12:34 CST · 论文身份与出版状态证据 DOIdoi.org · 2026-10-24 12:34 CST · 规范化论文身份

摘要（可展开，供朗读/NotebookLM）

论文题名

SJD++: Improved Speculative Jacobi Decoding for Training-Free Acceleration of Discrete Auto-Regressive Text-to-Image Generation

期刊：IEEE Transactions on Pattern Analysis and Machine Intelligence · DOI：10.1109/tpami.2026.3700227 · 语言：en · 摘要来源：出版方摘要

Large autoregressive models can generate high-quality, high-resolution images but suffer from slow generation speed, because these models require hundreds to thousands of sequential forward passes for next-token prediction during inference. To accelerate autoregressive text-to-image generation, we propose Speculative Jacobi Decoding++ (SJD++), a training-free probabilistic parallel decoding algorithm. Unlike traditional next-token prediction, SJD++ performs multi-token prediction in each forward pass, drastically reducing generation steps. Specifically, it integrates the iterative multi-token prediction mechanism from Jacobi decoding, with the probabilistic drafting-and-verification mechanism from speculative sampling. More importantly, for further acceleration, SJD++ reuses high-confidence draft tokens after each verification phase instead of resampling them all. We conduct extensive experiments on several representative autoregressive text-to-image generation models and demonstrate that SJD++ achieves 2× to 3× inference latency reduction and 2× to 7× step compression, while preserving visual quality with no observable degradation.

IEEE Transactions on Pattern Analysis and Machine Intelligence · FORMAL_ISSUE

Complete Fuzzy Knowledge Representation With Knowledge Graph Embedding

Haoning Li; Qinghua Huang

卷期
V48 · I10
门控
NEW_ARTICLE
DOI
10.1109/tpami.2026.3701743
证据层级
METADATA

官方论文页面www.computer.org · 2026-10-24 12:34 CST · 论文身份与出版状态证据 DOIdoi.org · 2026-10-24 12:34 CST · 规范化论文身份

摘要（可展开，供朗读/NotebookLM）

论文题名

Complete Fuzzy Knowledge Representation With Knowledge Graph Embedding

期刊：IEEE Transactions on Pattern Analysis and Machine Intelligence · DOI：10.1109/tpami.2026.3701743 · 语言：en · 摘要来源：出版方摘要

Fuzzy knowledge graph embedding (KGE) aims to represent uncertain facts for reliable reasoning and decision-making. While recent studies have begun to explore fine-grained fuzzy KGs that associate uncertainty with individual elements, existing approaches remain limited by i) unreliable neighborhood aggregation that may amplify low-confidence noise, ii) entangled semantic and reliability signals that obscure where uncertainty originates and hinder interpretability, and iii) restricted generality due to task- or metadata-specific designs. To address these issues, we propose FGF-GAT, a general encoder-decoder framework for fine-grained fuzzy KGE. On the encoder side, FGF-GAT employs a learnable neuro-fuzzy reasoning module for fine-grained fuzziness modeling, where fixed element-level memberships are transformed into reliability-aware signals through an ANFIS-inspired fuzzy parameterization and a lightweight TSK-style rule reasoning layer. These memberships are then injected into a rule-constrained fuzzy graph attention encoder for reliability-modulated neighborhood aggregation, thereby suppressing uncertainty-induced noise accumulation. On the decoder side, we develop a pluggable membership injection strategy and demonstrate that the framework is decoder-agnostic by instantiating a basic Fuzzy-TransE scorer and two stronger variants with DistMult and RotatE. Extensive experiments on four benchmarks under both entity prediction and relation prediction, together with ablation and sensitivity studies, consistently verify the effectiveness, robustness, and adaptability of the proposed framework.

Deep residual bidirectional learning for multi-horizon financial forecasting

Indu Rani; Shubhangi Porwal; Chandan Kumar Verma; Namita Srivastava; Manoj Jha

卷期
V19 · I5
门控
NEW_ARTICLE
DOI
10.1007/s12065-026-01273-5
证据层级
METADATA

官方论文页面link.springer.com · 2026-10-24 12:34 CST · 论文身份与出版状态证据 DOIdoi.org · 2026-10-24 12:34 CST · 规范化论文身份

摘要（可展开，供朗读/NotebookLM）

论文题名

Deep residual bidirectional learning for multi-horizon financial forecasting

期刊：Evolutionary Intelligence · DOI：10.1007/s12065-026-01273-5 · 语言：en · 摘要来源：出版方摘要

Forecasting broad-market indices is a challenging task due to the nonlinear, non-stationary, and noisy characteristics of financial time series. Recent geopolitical tensions, armed conflicts, and global economic uncertainties have further intensified market volatility, increasing the complexity of financial forecasting and highlighting the need for robust predictive models. This study proposes a Stacked Residual Bidirectional Long Short-Term Memory (S-ResBiLSTM) model for forecasting the S&P 500 index using data from November 2010 to February 2026. The proposed architecture combines residual learning with bidirectional temporal modeling to enhance feature representation, facilitate gradient propagation, and improve training stability in deep recurrent networks. In addition, a cosine-annealing learning-rate schedule is incorporated to promote efficient optimization and smooth convergence. The proposed model is evaluated against several deep learning baseline models using MAE, RMSE, and R². Experiments are conducted across multiple forecasting horizons and under noisy data conditions to assess predictive robustness and generalization capability. Furthermore, the contribution of the cosine-annealing strategy is systematically examined through comparative analysis. The statistical validity of the forecasting results is examined using the Diebold–Mariano test for pairwise model comparisons, while probability density analysis of actual and predicted values is conducted to evaluate the model’s ability to preserve the underlying distributional characteristics of the market data.

Evolutionary Intelligence · FORMAL_ISSUE

An efficient multilevel minimum cross entropy thresholding algorithm based on dynamic programming and divide-and-conquer search

Bo Lei; Songyi Shi

卷期
V19 · I5
门控
NEW_ARTICLE
DOI
10.1007/s12065-026-01268-2
证据层级
METADATA

官方论文页面link.springer.com · 2026-10-24 12:34 CST · 论文身份与出版状态证据 DOIdoi.org · 2026-10-24 12:34 CST · 规范化论文身份

摘要（可展开，供朗读/NotebookLM）

论文题名

An efficient multilevel minimum cross entropy thresholding algorithm based on dynamic programming and divide-and-conquer search

期刊：Evolutionary Intelligence · DOI：10.1007/s12065-026-01268-2 · 语言：en · 摘要来源：出版方摘要

Efficient calculation of the optimal thresholds for multilevel thresholding methods remains a challenging task. To obtain the optimal thresholds of the multilevel minimum cross entropy thresholding (MMCET) algorithm, this paper presents an efficient algorithm. First, we point out that the objective function of the MMCET algorithm has an optimal substructure, and the optimal thresholds of the MMCET algorithm can be calculated by the dynamic programming (DP) algorithm. Thresholds obtained by the DP algorithm are proven to be the global optimal thresholds. Second, it is proven that the class cost function of the MMCET method satisfies the property of the quadrilateral inequality. The computational efficiency of the MMCET algorithm based on DP can be improved further by a divide-and-conquer search algorithm. Finally, the performance of the MMCET algorithm based on DP and the divide-and-conquer algorithm is compared with that of the existing efficient MMCET methods and other image segmentation methods. Experiments show that the proposed algorithm can quickly obtain the global optimal thresholds and provide better segmentation results.

Multi-attribute decision-making for sustainable transportation selection in uncertain environments using the N-cubic fuzzy Hamacher aggregation operators

Tahir Abbas; Zill e Shams; Kashuf Javed; Muhammad Gulistan; Mohammed M. Al-Shamiri; Muhammad Usman Jamil

卷期
V19 · I5
门控
NEW_ARTICLE
DOI
10.1007/s12065-026-01269-1
证据层级
METADATA

官方论文页面link.springer.com · 2026-10-24 12:34 CST · 论文身份与出版状态证据 DOIdoi.org · 2026-10-24 12:34 CST · 规范化论文身份

摘要（可展开，供朗读/NotebookLM）

论文题名

Multi-attribute decision-making for sustainable transportation selection in uncertain environments using the N-cubic fuzzy Hamacher aggregation operators

期刊：Evolutionary Intelligence · DOI：10.1007/s12065-026-01269-1 · 语言：en · 摘要来源：出版方摘要

Multi-attribute decision-making (MADM) under uncertain and multidimensional information requires aggregation mechanisms capable of preserving the complex structure of expert evaluations. This research proposes a Hamacher-based aggregation framework within the N-cubic fuzzy set (NCFS) environment to give a flexible representation of uncertain transportation assessments. Based on Hamacher t-norm and t-conorm, new N-cubic fuzzy Hamacher weighted averaging (NCFHWA), N-cubic fuzzy Hamacher ordered weighted averaging (NCFHOWA), and N-cubic fuzzy Hamacher hybrid aggregation (NCFHHA) operators are formulated. The proposed operators are incorporated into an NCFS-based MADM procedure for sustainable transportation selection. A transportation case study involving five alternatives and four evaluation criteria demonstrates the application of the proposed framework. The obtained results identify Rail Transportation as the most preferred alternative, followed by the remaining transportation options, while Hydrogen Fuel Cell Trucks receives the lowest overall evaluation under the considered decision environment. A comparative analysis with the existing NCFWA and NCFOWA operators shows that the proposed operators produce slightly different score values while maintaining the same alternative ranking. Sensitivity analysis with respect to the Hamacher parameter further illustrates the behavior of the proposed operators under the investigated parameter settings. The study demonstrates the applicability of Hamacher aggregation within the NCFS framework for transportation MADM and provides a mathematical basis for handling uncertain expert evaluations.

Evolutionary Intelligence · FORMAL_ISSUE

Inertial and wearable sensor-based instrumented sit-to-stand assessment: a scoping review with consideration of multimodal approaches

Tomás Galhano; Saif Al-jumaili; Mário Lopes; Carlos Albuquerque; António D. Reis; Paulo Jorge Coelho; Diogo Luís Marques; Ivan Miguel Pires

卷期
V19 · I5
门控
NEW_ARTICLE
DOI
10.1007/s12065-026-01270-8
证据层级
METADATA

官方论文页面link.springer.com · 2026-10-24 12:34 CST · 论文身份与出版状态证据 DOIdoi.org · 2026-10-24 12:34 CST · 规范化论文身份

摘要（可展开，供朗读/NotebookLM）

论文题名

Inertial and wearable sensor-based instrumented sit-to-stand assessment: a scoping review with consideration of multimodal approaches

期刊：Evolutionary Intelligence · DOI：10.1007/s12065-026-01270-8 · 语言：en · 摘要来源：出版方摘要

Sit-to-stand (STS) tests are widely used to assess functional mobility, but conventional stopwatch scoring mainly captures completion time or repetition count. This scoping review mapped evidence on instrumented STS assessment using inertial and wearable sensing, including multimodal systems incorporating these technologies. Following JBI guidance and PRISMA-ScR, PubMed/MEDLINE, IEEE Xplore, Scopus, and Web of Science were searched on 9 April 2026 for studies published from 2013 to 2025. Eligible primary studies evaluated human STS, stand-to-sit, or chair-rise performance using objective sensing or digital analysis technologies and reported quantitative STS-specific outcomes. Seventeen primary studies published between 2019 and 2025 were included. Fourteen used inertial or wearable sensing, and three used multimodal systems combining inertial or wearable sensors with complementary visual, force, or pressure measurements. Reported outcomes included total and phase duration, linear and angular motion, estimated power, force or pressure variables, movement trajectories, smoothness, variability, asymmetry, and postural stabilization. Applications included fall-risk evaluation, frailty and sarcopenia screening, neurological and cognitive assessment, rehabilitation-related assessment, and home monitoring. Machine learning and automated analysis were used in several applications, although independent and external validation remained limited. Instrumented STS assessment can provide information on how the movement is performed beyond conventional stopwatch measures. Further clinical translation requires standardized protocols, transparent feature definitions, subject-wise and external validation, complete system reporting, and prospective evaluation in diverse populations.

Effective metaheuristics for the distributed hybrid flow shop problem with identical parallel machines and setup times

Achraf Sayah; Said Aqil; Mohamed Lahby

卷期
V19 · I5
门控
METADATA_CHANGED
DOI
10.1007/s12065-026-01263-7
证据层级
METADATA

官方论文页面link.springer.com · 2026-10-24 12:34 CST · 论文身份与出版状态证据 DOIdoi.org · 2026-10-24 12:34 CST · 规范化论文身份

摘要（可展开，供朗读/NotebookLM）

论文题名

Effective metaheuristics for the distributed hybrid flow shop problem with identical parallel machines and setup times

期刊：Evolutionary Intelligence · DOI：10.1007/s12065-026-01263-7 · 语言：en · 摘要来源：出版方摘要

This paper investigates the Distributed Hybrid Flow Shop Scheduling Problem with Identical Parallel Machines and Sequence-Dependent Setup Times (DHFSP-IPM-SDST), a complex and realistic extension of the classical flow shop model that is highly relevant to modern distributed manufacturing environments. To address this scheduling problem, we propose both exact and approximate methods. First, a mixed-integer linear programming (MILP) and Constraint Programming (CP) models are proposed to optimally solve small-sized instances and to serve as a validation benchmark. For larger instances, a set of hybrid methods is developed, combining priority rules, constructive heuristics, and three metaheuristics, namely Population-based Iterative Local Search (PILS), a Self-Recombination Genetic Algorithm (SRGA), and a Hybrid Artificial Bee Colony algorithm (HABC). The proposed metaheuristics incorporate problem-specific mechanisms, including critical factory focused neighborhood search, feasibility-preserving crossover, and inter- and intra-factory search procedures. A comprehensive computational study is conducted on a diverse set of benchmark instances to evaluate and compare the effectiveness of the proposed approaches. The results demonstrate that the proposed methods consistently generate high-quality solutions within reasonable computational times. Among the compared approaches, the HABC algorithm achieves the best overall performance in terms of solution quality and robustness, while maintaining competitive computational efficiency.

Normal cloud similarity based on Fisher–Rao distance and its applications

Changlin Xu; Yulin Chen

卷期
V19 · I5
门控
METADATA_CHANGED
DOI
10.1007/s12065-026-01265-5
证据层级
METADATA

官方论文页面link.springer.com · 2026-10-24 12:34 CST · 论文身份与出版状态证据 DOIdoi.org · 2026-10-24 12:34 CST · 规范化论文身份

摘要（可展开，供朗读/NotebookLM）

论文题名

Normal cloud similarity based on Fisher–Rao distance and its applications

期刊：Evolutionary Intelligence · DOI：10.1007/s12065-026-01265-5 · 语言：en · 摘要来源：出版方摘要

To address the problems of high computational complexity and weak discriminative ability in existing cloud model similarity measures, this paper proposes a Fisher–Rao distance-based similarity measure for normal clouds, named FRCM. Existing similarity measures often struggle to balance computational efficiency and discriminative capability: overlap-based methods usually involve numerical integration, while some vector-based methods may not sufficiently capture the distributional differences between cloud models. This paper uses entropy–expectation curve clusters under the 3 σ principle to describe the distribution characteristics of cloud models, and introduces the Fisher–Rao distance to construct a similarity measure for normal clouds. The proposed method performs weighted aggregation of distances under different envelope coefficients and then normalizes the aggregated distance. In this way, it avoids the numerical integration required by traditional methods while comprehensively considering the numerical characteristics of the cloud model. Numerical simulations and time series classification experiments are conducted to compare the proposed method with existing similarity measures. The results show that FRCM achieves strong discriminative ability with low computational cost. Further comparison with distance-based methods demonstrates that FRCM attains competitive classification accuracy, confirming its feasibility and effectiveness in practical applications.

Evolutionary Intelligence · FORMAL_ASSIGNMENT

A constraint-aware adaptive pigeon-inspired optimization algorithm for the green pickup and delivery problem with time windows

Zhilin Liu; Yuelin Gao; Huimin Wang; Mengqi Jiang

卷期
V19 · I5
门控
METADATA_CHANGED
DOI
10.1007/s12065-026-01264-6
证据层级
METADATA

官方论文页面link.springer.com · 2026-10-24 12:34 CST · 论文身份与出版状态证据 DOIdoi.org · 2026-10-24 12:34 CST · 规范化论文身份

摘要（可展开，供朗读/NotebookLM）

论文题名

A constraint-aware adaptive pigeon-inspired optimization algorithm for the green pickup and delivery problem with time windows

期刊：Evolutionary Intelligence · DOI：10.1007/s12065-026-01264-6 · 语言：en · 摘要来源：出版方摘要

With the rapid growth of e-commerce reverse logistics, fresh cold-chain distribution, and urban freight services, pickup-and-delivery routing has become increasingly complex. Vehicles are required to satisfy pickup-delivery pairing, time-window, and capacity constraints simultaneously, which greatly increases the difficulty of constructing feasible and high-quality routes. Meanwhile, carbon emissions generated during transportation further require routing decisions to account for low-carbon operation. To address these issues, this study investigates the Green Pickup-and-Delivery Problem with Time Windows (GPDPTW). A hierarchical optimization model is developed by incorporating vehicle usage, route length, service-related indicators, and carbon emissions into the objective structure. To solve the model, an Adaptive Multi-Neighborhood Pigeon-Inspired Optimization (AMPIO) algorithm is proposed. The algorithm combines multiple neighborhood operators, a dynamic operator selection mechanism, and a staged search strategy to maintain pickup-delivery and time-window feasibility while improving route quality and emission performance. Computational experiments on 29 benchmark instances show that AMPIO obtains vehicle-count and travel-distance results comparable to representative state-of-the-art PDPTW methods, while additionally incorporating carbon emissions and service-related objectives. A real-world case study in Yinchuan further demonstrates that, compared with the original Pigeon-Inspired Optimization (PIO) algorithm, AMPIO reduces total travel distance by 3.0%, carbon emissions by 2.8%, and transportation cost by 1.2%, confirming its practical applicability for green urban pickup-and-delivery routing.

Evolutionary Intelligence · FORMAL_ASSIGNMENT

Survival probability-weighted clustering for UAV-based multi-robot task allocation in disaster response

Abdullah Osman; Tarık Küçükdeniz

摘要（可展开，供朗读/NotebookLM）

论文题名

Survival probability-weighted clustering for UAV-based multi-robot task allocation in disaster response

期刊：Evolutionary Intelligence · DOI：10.1007/s12065-026-01257-5 · 语言：en · 摘要来源：出版方摘要

In disaster response, effective allocation of search and rescue UAVs to survivors is critical for maximizing survival rates, yet existing clustering-based multi-robot task allocation (MRTA) methods rely predominantly on spatial distance criteria, ignoring time-dependent health deterioration. This paper proposes P-MWFCM-ACO, a two-phase framework that integrates, for the first time, priority weights derived from an empirically validated survival probability model into the clustering phase of MRTA. A modified weighted fuzzy c-means (MWFCM) algorithm directs cluster centers toward regions with the most critically injured survivors; ant colony optimization then determines efficient routing within each cluster. Computational experiments comprising 1,320 simulation runs on the Loggi-n901-k42 benchmark dataset with 11 UAVs across four speed conditions (5–50 m/s) demonstrate that MWFCM significantly outperforms both k-means and standard fuzzy c-means in total survival probability (p < 0.001 at all speeds), achieving 10–43% lower health deterioration versus k-means and 7–45% versus FCM, at the cost of modestly longer routes (4–6%). Additional experiments on three further instances with different scales and spatial structures, including a weighted k-means baseline, confirm this advantage at 30–50 m/s on every instance and at 10 m/s on three of four, and delineate its boundary: at low speeds on large, strongly clustered layouts, unweighted clustering can retain more survival. A further comparison against representative non-clustering allocation methods—a market-based sequential auction and a direct mTSP genetic algorithm—shows that MWFCM outperforms both at every tested speed on the primary benchmark, at a fraction of the direct method’s computation, and that a clustering method ranks first in every tested condition. Analysis of the underlying mechanism reveals that MWFCM achieves these gains by redistributing UAV resources toward higher-priority areas, producing a highly favorable trade-off between improved preservation where it matters most and negligible cost elsewhere. These results provide the first empirical evidence that integrating medical triage models into the clustering phase of MRTA significantly improves rescue outcomes in mass casualty scenarios, suggesting that cross-domain integration of health-based criteria into multi-robot coordination merits further investigation.

Intelligent agents: architectures, learning paradigms, deployment models, and applications

Elias Dritsas; Maria Trigka

卷期
V19 · I5
门控
METADATA_CHANGED
DOI
10.1007/s12065-026-01259-3
证据层级
METADATA

官方论文页面link.springer.com · 2026-10-24 12:34 CST · 论文身份与出版状态证据 DOIdoi.org · 2026-10-24 12:34 CST · 规范化论文身份

摘要（可展开，供朗读/NotebookLM）

论文题名

Intelligent agents: architectures, learning paradigms, deployment models, and applications

期刊：Evolutionary Intelligence · DOI：10.1007/s12065-026-01259-3 · 语言：en · 摘要来源：出版方摘要

Intelligent agents have emerged as a fundamental paradigm for enabling autonomous, adaptive, and data-driven decision-making across increasingly heterogeneous computational environments. Despite substantial advances in learning algorithms, agent architectures, and deployment infrastructures, the literature remains fragmented across methodological, architectural, and application-specific perspectives. To address this fragmentation, this survey develops a unified taxonomy that systematically integrates learning paradigms, agent architectures, coordination mechanisms, deployment models, application domains, and evaluation frameworks from a common analytical perspective. The present work synthesizes recent developments in reinforcement learning (RL), federated learning (FL), neuro-symbolic reasoning, transfer learning (TL), evolutionary computation, and meta-learning, while examining their interactions with reactive, deliberative, hybrid, hierarchical, and cognitive agent architectures, as well as communication and coordination mechanisms in multi-agent systems (MAS). Particular attention is devoted to the relationship between learning mechanisms, deployment environments, and operational requirements in resource-constrained, dynamic, and large-scale systems. Beyond characterizing intelligent agent technologies, this survey provides a structured analysis of representative benchmarks, evaluation metrics, domain-specific performance indicators, and emerging challenges related to scalability, robustness, trustworthiness, interoperability, energy efficiency, and the development of embodied and large language model (LLM)-based agent systems. By linking methodological foundations with practical deployment considerations across domains such as robotics, healthcare, finance, cybersecurity, smart cities, education, and scientific computing, this study offers a comprehensive cross-domain synthesis of the intelligent agent landscape. The resulting framework facilitates systematic comparison, identifies recurring design trade-offs and research gaps, and provides a structured foundation for future advances in intelligent agent systems.

Dynamic Bayesian Gaussian mixture model based decision variable grouping for large-scale multi-objective optimization

Zhiping Tan; Zhenhua Xue; Yu Tang; Mingwei Fang; Huasheng Huang

卷期
V19 · I5

证据层级
METADATA

官方论文页面link.springer.com · 2026-10-24 12:34 CST · 论文身份与出版状态证据 DOIdoi.org · 2026-10-24 12:34 CST · 规范化论文身份

摘要（可展开，供朗读/NotebookLM）

论文题名

Dynamic Bayesian Gaussian mixture model based decision variable grouping for large-scale multi-objective optimization

期刊：Evolutionary Intelligence · DOI：10.1007/s12065-026-01260-w · 语言：en · 摘要来源：出版方摘要

Large-scale multi-objective optimization problems (LSMOPs) typically involve hundreds or thousands of decision variables, posing significant challenges for evolutionary algorithms in balancing convergence efficiency and population diversity. To address these challenges, this paper proposes a dynamic Bayesian Gaussian mixture model based decision variable grouping evolutionary algorithm (TDGEA). First, a dynamic Bayesian Gaussian mixture model framework is employed to formulate the problem and perform preliminary grouping of decision variables, which categorizes variables into convergence-related variables and diversity-related variables. Then, a dynamic differential grouping strategy is introduced to further partition variables according to their interactions, ensuring strong intra-group coupling and weak inter-group coupling. Finally, an adaptive dual-population cooperative optimization strategy is adopted to independently optimize convergence-related and diversity-related variables. Experimental evaluations on standard benchmark test suites show that the proposed TDGEA consistently outperforms several state-of-the-art large-scale optimization algorithms with respect to convergence speed, optimization accuracy, and scalability.

Advanced YOLOv10n - based approach for efficient agricultural pest detection

Fengzhi Dai; Jiabin Chen; Hongshuo Zhai; Yuliang Liu

卷期
V19 · I5
门控

10.1007/s12065-026-01261-9
证据层级
METADATA

官方论文页面link.springer.com · 2026-10-24 12:34 CST · 论文身份与出版状态证据 DOIdoi.org · 2026-10-24 12:34 CST · 规范化论文身份

摘要（可展开，供朗读/NotebookLM）

论文题名

Advanced YOLOv10n - based approach for efficient agricultural pest detection

期刊：Evolutionary Intelligence · DOI：10.1007/s12065-026-01261-9 · 语言：en · 摘要来源：出版方摘要

In agricultural production, pest detection plays a critical role. Traditional pest monitoring primarily relies on manual inspection, which is inefficient and highly subjective, making it inadequate for the requirements of modern precision agriculture. With the rapid advancement of deep learning techniques in image recognition and related fields, their application in pest detection has demonstrated significant potential. To address this demand, this paper proposes an efficient pest detection method based on the YOLOv10n deep learning model. To overcome the limitations of YOLOv10n, including insufficient multi-scale feature fusion, information bottlenecks, and weak local feature representation capability, this study introduces a re-parameterized general feature pyramid network (RepGFPN) and a spatial pyramid pooling enhanced local attention network (SPPELAN), further optimized by integrating the efficient channel attention (ECA) mechanism. The proposed model is trained and evaluated on the IP102 dataset, while the Pest24 dataset is employed to validate its generalization capability. Experimental results demonstrate that, with relative increases of 19.3% in parameters and 9% in GFLOPs, the proposed model achieves improved detection accuracy. Specifically, on the IP102 dataset, the model attains an mAP@0.5 of 0.612, representing a 2.5% improvement over the original YOLOv10n, and an mAP@0.5:0.95 of 0.434, with an increase of 1.2 percentage points. Compared with YOLOv10n, YOLOv11n and the latest YOLOv26n, the proposed YOLOv10n-RS-ECA model achieves better overall performance on most pest categories of the tested datasets. Notably, for Cnaphalocrocis medinalis (rice leaf roller), the mAP@0.5 reaches 0.782, which is 40.9% higher than YOLOv10n, 11.2% higher than YOLOv11n, and 27.8% higher than YOLOv26n on the IP102 dataset. The proposed model provides a feasible technical reference for agricultural pest detection and identification. On the Pest24 dataset, the overall mAP@0.5 reaches 0.604, achieving a 1.4% improvement over the baseline YOLOv10n, thereby demonstrating strong generalization capability. The proposed model provides reliable support for agricultural pest detection and identification. Future work will focus on model compression and acceleration techniques to meet real-time detection requirements, as well as the integration of multimodal data to further enhance detection accuracy and robustness.

Optimization strategies for variational quantum algorithms in noisy landscapes

Vojtěch Novák; Ivan Zelinka; Václav Snášel

卷期
V19 · I5

证据层级
METADATA

官方论文页面link.springer.com · 2026-10-24 12:34 CST · 论文身份与出版状态证据 DOIdoi.org · 2026-10-24 12:34 CST · 规范化论文身份

摘要（可展开，供朗读/NotebookLM）

论文题名

Optimization strategies for variational quantum algorithms in noisy landscapes

期刊：Evolutionary Intelligence · DOI：10.1007/s12065-026-01248-6 · 语言：en · 摘要来源：出版方摘要

Variational quantum algorithms are a leading approach to NISQ-era computation, but finite-shot measurements make their optimization stochastic. We benchmark more than fifty metaheuristic algorithms on a multi-phase benchmark covering Ising and 192-parameter Hubbard models using a hardware-efficient TwoLocal ansatz in a shot-noise-only setting, with finite-shot sampling but without hardware-specific coherent or decoherence errors. Standard differential evolution and local optimizers (COBYLA and SPSA) perform well in noiseless simulations but degrade or stagnate under finite-shot sampling. Among the tested methods, CMA-ES and adaptive DE variants, particularly iL-SHADE, were the most reliable on the noisy objectives. These results limit the generality of standard DE findings obtained from statevector simulations and indicate that adaptive parameter control may improve robustness to finite-shot noise.

Control-oriented multi-objective optimization for percussive massage robots using an enhanced NSGA-II

Jie Xu; Huan Liu; Zhifeng Wang

卷期
V19 · I5
门控
METADATA_CHANGED
DOI
10.1007/s12065-026-01258-4
证据层级
METADATA

官方论文页面link.springer.com · 2026-10-24 12:34 CST · 论文身份与出版状态证据 DOIdoi.org · 2026-10-24 12:34 CST · 规范化论文身份

摘要（可展开，供朗读/NotebookLM）

论文题名

Control-oriented multi-objective optimization for percussive massage robots using an enhanced NSGA-II

期刊：Evolutionary Intelligence · DOI：10.1007/s12065-026-01258-4 · 语言：en · 摘要来源：出版方摘要

Percussive massage motions impose stringent requirements on efficiency and joint-level impact suppression. To address this problem, a control-oriented multi-objective trajectory optimization framework is developed. Motion rhythm is regulated at the joint-trajectory level by jointly optimizing the spatial position and time allocation of intermediate peak points in quintic B-spline trajectories, enabling an explicit trade-off between efficiency and impact mitigation. For safety-sensitive percussive massage applications, a diversity-aware evolutionary search framework is developed. An EMA-guided diversity stabilization mechanism is employed to smooth adaptive parameter evolution, while adaptive crossover regulation and SOA-inspired local refinement are coordinated within NSGA-II to balance exploration and exploitation and improve Pareto-front distribution. This enables the generation of diverse time–impact trade-off trajectories that can accommodate different massage intensity and safety requirements. ZDT benchmark evaluations demonstrate significant improvements in solution diversity and convergence quality. Simulation studies conducted in CoppeliaSim further show that the optimized trajectories reduce joint impact indicators by 53.6%–79.5% while maintaining comparable execution time, confirming the effectiveness of the proposed framework for percussive robotic motion control. Additional contact-dynamics validation using a Kelvin–Voigt tissue interaction model further confirms consistent reductions in contact-force responses under soft-tissue, stiff-tissue, and bone-contact conditions. The results demonstrate the effectiveness and robustness of the proposed framework for safety-oriented percussive robotic motion planning.

MEPO-SLM: multi-objective evolutionary prompt optimization for energy-efficient small language models on edge devices

Yousef Sanjalawe; Salam Al-E’mari; Sharif Naser Makhadmeh

卷期
V19 · I5
门控
METADATA_CHANGED
DOI
10.1007/s12065-026-01255-7
证据层级
METADATA

官方论文页面link.springer.com · 2026-10-24 12:34 CST · 论文身份与出版状态证据 DOIdoi.org · 2026-10-24 12:34 CST · 规范化论文身份

摘要（可展开，供朗读/NotebookLM）

论文题名

MEPO-SLM: multi-objective evolutionary prompt optimization for energy-efficient small language models on edge devices

期刊：Evolutionary Intelligence · DOI：10.1007/s12065-026-01255-7 · 语言：en · 摘要来源：出版方摘要

Deploying small language models (SLMs) on edge hardware requires simultaneously meeting four conflicting requirements: high task accuracy, low hallucination rate, bounded inference latency, and minimal energy consumption. We present MEPO-SLM, a framework that reformulates prompt engineering for SLMs as a four-objective Pareto problem over task inaccuracy ( 𝑓 1 ), hallucination rate ( 𝑓 2 ), per-query latency ( 𝑓 3 ), and per-query energy ( 𝑓 4 ). Prompts are represented as five-segment genomes, varied by four coordinated operators, including a directed length operator and a Hallucination-Aware Perturbation repair operator, and kept diverse in objective and prompt-embedding space. We evaluate Phi-3-mini and Gemma-2B on English TriviaQA and Arabic medical QA, and TinyLlama-1.1B on TriviaQA only, using Raspberry Pi 5 and NVIDIA Jetson Orin Nano measurements. Across the reported settings, MEPO-SLM improves accuracy by up to 12.3 percentage points, reduces hallucination by up to 41.2% relative, and reduces per-query energy by up to 28.4%. On the reference Phi-3-mini/TriviaQA setting, hypervolume is 57.3% higher than MOPrompt, the strongest bi-objective baseline, and 43.7% higher than GEPA, the strongest non-MEPO-SLM comparator. Statistical tests are restricted to explicitly repeated, paired experiments; single-run hardware results are reported descriptively. To our knowledge, no prior APO framework jointly optimizes all four objectives on edge hardware for both English and Arabic NLP.

Large language model enhanced heuristic synergy orchestrator for adaptive operator evolution

Hao-Ran Sun; Ruo-Bin Wang; Lin Xu

卷期
V19 · I5
门控
METADATA_CHANGED
DOI
10.1007/s12065-026-01249-5
证据层级
METADATA

官方论文页面link.springer.com · 2026-10-24 12:34 CST · 论文身份与出版状态证据 DOIdoi.org · 2026-10-24 12:34 CST · 规范化论文身份

摘要（可展开，供朗读/NotebookLM）

论文题名

Large language model enhanced heuristic synergy orchestrator for adaptive operator evolution

期刊：Evolutionary Intelligence · DOI：10.1007/s12065-026-01249-5 · 语言：en · 摘要来源：出版方摘要

Traditional evolutionary computation has long been constrained by the fundamental limitations of static parameter control strategy selection. To address this bottleneck, reinforcement learning (RL) strategies have been introduced to achieve dynamic parameter adaptation. Yet, these RL-based strategies adapt parameters but not search logics; they rearrange predefined operators without ever transcending them to construct novel, landscape-tailored paradigms. To overcome this challenge, this paper proposes a hierarchical intelligent optimization framework and introduces Large Language Models (LLMs) as strategy optimizers, thereby proposing the LLM-HSO algorithm. This algorithm leverages the contextual understanding and code generation capabilities of LLMs to perform evaluation and strategy reconstruction during the evolutionary process. Experimental results demonstrate that the proposed algorithm exhibits excellent convergence speed and optimization accuracy on the CEC2022 and CEC2017 benchmark functions, validating its potential as an LLM-enhanced evolutionary computation paradigm. Furthermore, we applied the LLM-HSO algorithm to optimize multi-robot collaborative path planning problems, confirming its ability to tackle complex optimization challenges and its potential as a reliable optimizer in practical engineering applications.

An efficient intrusion detection system for securing MQTT-based IoT environment using particle swarm optimization, LGBM and XGB

Asimkiran Dandapat; Bhaskar Mondal

卷期
V19 · I5
门控
METADATA_CHANGED
DOI
10.1007/s12065-026-01256-6
证据层级
METADATA

官方论文页面link.springer.com · 2026-10-24 12:34 CST · 论文身份与出版状态证据 DOIdoi.org · 2026-10-24 12:34 CST · 规范化论文身份

摘要（可展开，供朗读/NotebookLM）

论文题名

An efficient intrusion detection system for securing MQTT-based IoT environment using particle swarm optimization, LGBM and XGB

期刊：Evolutionary Intelligence · DOI：10.1007/s12065-026-01256-6 · 语言：en · 摘要来源：出版方摘要

'Security' is a primary concern for Internet of Things (IoT) devices due to their diverse applications. Potential attacks in these applications are constantly emerging and evolving. Moreover, it creates a need to update the existing Machine Learning (ML)- based Intrusion Detection System (IDS) to address these challenges. Of late, many lightweight protocols have been proposed for communication in these devices. Among them, Message Queuing Telemetry Transport (MQTT) has gained popularity for its low memory consumption and minimal bandwidth requirements. However, this protocol lacks built-in encryption, authentication, and access control mechanisms, which pose a threat to secure communications. This research presents an efficient IDS framework integrating metaheuristic feature selection, i.e. Particle Swarm Optimization (PSO), eXtreme Gradient Boosting (XGB), and Light Gradient Boosting Machine (LGBM), known as advanced ensemble learners to classify benign and attack scenarios in MQTT-based IoT environments. PSO overcomes local optima traps in feature selection, identifying the most critical parameters for traffic classification. In this approach, optimized features have been processed using a dual-ensemble classifier like XGB, selected for its rigorous L1/L2 regularization that creates an ability to handle network-induced data sparsity. Whereas, LGBM aids in its leaf-wise growth strategy and high-speed execution at the IoT edge. Experimental results on the MQTT-IoT-IDS2020 dataset indicate that the proposed XGB-PSO model have achieved an exceptional precision of 99.98% and accuracy of 90.14%, while the LGBM-PSO model have achieved a precision of 99.31% and accuracy of 90.15%. To validate the generalizability of this framework against modern threats, these models being tested on an alternative benchmark dataset DoS/DDoS-MQTT-IoT. Validation attributes that the XGB-PSO achieves commendable accuracy (91.23%)and precision (96.95%), while the LGBM-PSO model meets 89.62% and 96.03%, respectively. Interpretability and robustness of the model are ensured through SHapley Additive Explanations (SHAP)-based feature attribution and Synthetic Minority Oversampling Technique (SMOTE)-driven imbalance mitigation, to validate the performance of the proposed framework. These findings clearly show that combining metaheuristic feature refinement with an ensemble approach could be an exciting choice for authoritative and adaptable solutions in modern IoT attack vectors.

Digital Twin architectures for remote monitoring and control in CNC manufacturing systems: a systematic review

Omar A. Hashem; A. M. Bassiuny; Hussein M. A. Hussein; Ghada A. Elgamal

卷期
V19 · I5
门控

证据层级
METADATA

官方论文页面link.springer.com · 2026-10-24 12:34 CST · 论文身份与出版状态证据 DOIdoi.org · 2026-10-24 12:34 CST · 规范化论文身份

摘要（可展开，供朗读/NotebookLM）

论文题名

Digital Twin architectures for remote monitoring and control in CNC manufacturing systems: a systematic review

期刊：Evolutionary Intelligence · DOI：10.1007/s12065-026-01254-8 · 语言：en · 摘要来源：出版方摘要

Digital Twin (DT) technology has become a key enabler of smart manufacturing; however, its application in Computer Numerical Control (CNC) systems remains largely limited to monitoring and predictive tasks rather than real-time control. This limitation is particularly critical for remote operation of multi-axis CNC machines, where latency and synchronization directly affect control performance. This paper presents a systematic review of Digital Twin architectures for CNC machine tools, focusing on their suitability for remote monitoring and control. A PRISMA-based methodology is adopted to ensure a transparent and reproducible study selection process. The selected works are analyzed from an architectural perspective, considering system structure, communication protocols, synchronization strategies, and control integration. The results indicate that most existing implementations are not designed for time-critical closed-loop control and exhibit significant latency constraints. Cloud-based architectures typically introduce delays ranging from tens to hundreds of milliseconds, whereas edge-based approaches reduce latency to a few milliseconds, highlighting a trade-off between scalability and real-time performance. The study identifies key research gaps and emphasizes the need for hybrid edge–cloud Digital Twin architectures capable of supporting reliable and low-latency remote CNC control.

SMOG: an adaptive hybrid oversampling framework using SMOTE and conditional GAN for difficulty-aware learning on imbalanced data

Jatinder Kaur; Vimal Parmar; B. Koteswara Rao; Arikatla Jaya Lakshmi; V. Vivek; Sarbeswara Hota; B. Ankayarkanni; Devendra Singh; Apurav Gautam

卷期
V19 · I5

证据层级
METADATA

官方论文页面link.springer.com · 2026-10-24 12:34 CST · 论文身份与出版状态证据 DOIdoi.org · 2026-10-24 12:34 CST · 规范化论文身份

摘要（可展开，供朗读/NotebookLM）

论文题名

SMOG: an adaptive hybrid oversampling framework using SMOTE and conditional GAN for difficulty-aware learning on imbalanced data

期刊：Evolutionary Intelligence · DOI：10.1007/s12065-026-01253-9 · 语言：en · 摘要来源：出版方摘要

Imbalanced data classification remains a persistent challenge in machine learning, often leading to biased decision boundaries and poor minority-class recognition. To address this issue, we propose SMOG, an adaptive hybrid oversampling framework that integrates the Synthetic Minority Over-sampling Technique (SMOTE) with a Conditional Generative Adversarial Network (GAN)-based difficulty-aware learning strategy. The proposed approach incorporates a difficulty-aware SVM margin estimation mechanism to quantify the learning complexity of individual samples. A contrastive difficulty scoring scheme further categorizes minority instances into easy, moderate, and hard samples based on their proximity to majority-class regions. Guided by this adaptive difficulty assessment, density-aware SMOTE is applied to moderately difficult samples, while a difficulty-conditioned GAN (D-GAN) is employed to generate high-quality synthetic data for highly challenging instances. This intelligent sample-generation strategy dynamically enhances minority representation while preserving class boundary integrity. Experimental evaluations on benchmark UCI datasets demonstrate that SMOG consistently outperforms traditional oversampling and existing GAN-based methods in terms of minority-class detection, F1-score, and overall classification robustness. The proposed framework highlights the effectiveness of adaptive hybrid generative strategies for intelligent learning on imbalanced data.

Evolutionary and learning-based many-objective routing in wireless sensor networks via time-varying NSGA-III and deep Q-learning

Mohamed Rahal; Issam Zidi; Nejah Nasri

卷期
V19 · I5
门控
METADATA_CHANGED
DOI
10.1007/s12065-026-01251-x
证据层级
METADATA

官方论文页面link.springer.com · 2026-10-24 12:34 CST · 论文身份与出版状态证据 DOIdoi.org · 2026-10-24 12:34 CST · 规范化论文身份

摘要（可展开，供朗读/NotebookLM）

论文题名

Evolutionary and learning-based many-objective routing in wireless sensor networks via time-varying NSGA-III and deep Q-learning

期刊：Evolutionary Intelligence · DOI：10.1007/s12065-026-01251-x · 语言：en · 摘要来源：出版方摘要

Multi-objective evolutionary algorithms (MOEAs) are powerful tools for solving complex optimization problems. Non-dominated Sorting Genetic Algorithm III (NSGA-III) is one of the most widely recognized methods for solving many-objective optimization problems. However, the static reference point mechanism limits its ability to adapt to dynamic environments. To address this limitation, the first contribution of this paper is the introduction of TV-NSGA-III, a novel time-varying version of NSGA-III. It incorporates adaptive reference point adjustment mechanisms to improve flexibility and adaptability to changing problem spaces. Then, we apply it in combination with Deep Q-Learning (DQL) to create a novel framework in the context of Wireless Sensor Networks (WSNs). In WSNs, effective routing becomes increasingly important as they grow and become more complex. The proposed TV-NSGA-III is applied to address this routing problem, modeled as a many-objective optimization problem. It generates initial routing paths by optimizing six conflicting objectives. During the routing process, DQL is applied to refine these initial routes in real-time by adapting to dynamic network changes. TV-NSGA-III is compared with eight state-of-the-art many-objective algorithms for solving dynamic optimization problems. It achieves the best average Mean Inverted Generational Distance (0.02325−0.05383) and Mean Hypervolume (0.97185−0.93785) across 3–7 objectives. TV-NSGA-III-DQL is compared to four state-of-the-art approaches for WSN routing. It achieves an average Packet Delivery Ratio of 93.75%, which ensures reliable data transmission. It also achieves a lower average transmission latency of 2.3s and increases average residual energy by 32.3% over the compared approaches. These improvements have been confirmed through statistical tests.

Fractional-fuzzy modeling of memory-dependent retrial queueing systems with working vacation and threshold control

M. Lakshmi; N. Micheal Mathavavisakan

摘要（可展开，供朗读/NotebookLM）

论文题名

Fractional-fuzzy modeling of memory-dependent retrial queueing systems with working vacation and threshold control

期刊：Evolutionary Intelligence · DOI：10.1007/s12065-026-01252-w · 语言：en · 摘要来源：出版方摘要

In this paper, we investigate a novel fractional-fuzzy retrial queueing model with working vacation, balking, feedback and server breakdowns, motivated by performance optimization in cloud storage systems. Specifically, the model integrates three advanced analytical frameworks: (i) fuzzy set theory with triangular membership functions and 𝛼 -cut decomposition to capture epistemic uncertainty in arrival, service, retrial and failure parameters; (ii) Caputo fractional derivatives of order 𝜅 ∈ ( 0 , 1 ] to encode memory-dependent dynamics and non-Markovian transient behavior and (iii) an M-policy threshold activation mechanism to balance energy efficiency against congestion risk. Using the supplementary variable method and probability generating function technique, we derive explicit interval-valued steady-state distributions for the orbital queue length and server state probabilities. Furthermore, key performance metrics - including mean system size, waiting time, throughput, availability and energy proxy - are obtained in closed form via 𝛼 -cut interval arithmetic. Moreover, a comprehensive cost function aggregating holding, operational and provisioning expenditures is minimized, with numerical results confirming convexity and global optimality. Sensitivity analysis reveals that while fuzzy parameter bounds contract monotonically with confidence level 𝛼 , fractional memory profoundly impacts transient dynamics, significantly prolonging the relaxation time to steady-state compared to classical Markovian models. Additionally, the proposed framework is illustrated through a numerical study motivated by cloud storage systems, demonstrating that robust capacity provisioning under worst-case fuzzy realizations requires 𝜇 ≥ 1.8 𝜆 to guarantee stability. Consequently, the managerial insights emphasize transient congestion management by recommending proactive capacity scaling for finite-horizon operations under strong memory effects ( 𝜅 ≤ 0.5 ), along with robust capacity provisioning and uncertainty-budgeted service-level agreements. Ultimately, this work establishes a unified analytical foundation for designing resilient, cost-efficient service systems under simultaneous parameter ambiguity and temporal dependence.

Robust counterfactual explanations under model multiplicity using multi-objective optimization

Keita Kinjo

卷期
V19 · I5
门控
METADATA_CHANGED

10.1007/s12065-026-01244-w
证据层级
METADATA

官方论文页面link.springer.com · 2026-10-24 12:34 CST · 论文身份与出版状态证据 DOIdoi.org · 2026-10-24 12:34 CST · 规范化论文身份

摘要（可展开，供朗读/NotebookLM）

论文题名

Robust counterfactual explanations under model multiplicity using multi-objective optimization

期刊：Evolutionary Intelligence · DOI：10.1007/s12065-026-01244-w · 语言：en · 摘要来源：出版方摘要

In recent years, explainability in machine learning has gained importance. In this context, counterfactual explanation (CE), which presents the minimal change to input features required to alter a model’s prediction to a desired target, has attracted attention. However, it has been pointed out that CE is not robust when there are multiple machine-learning models with similar accuracy (model multiplicity). Since users should not be penalized by an arbitrary model choice, generating CEs that remain stable across models is essential for reliable decision-making under model multiplicity. In this paper, we propose robust CEs that introduce a new viewpoint—Pareto improvement—and a method that uses multi-objective optimization to generate them. To evaluate the proposed method, we conducted experiments using both simulated and real data. Real-data experiments on two domains—web search trend data with continuous features and educational intervention data with binary features—demonstrate that the proposed method produces robust CEs under model multiplicity and is applicable to diverse real-world settings. This study highlights the potential of ensuring robustness in decision-making by applying the concept of social welfare. We believe that this research can serve as a valuable foundation for various fields, including explainability in machine learning, decision-making, and action planning based on machine learning.

A multi-strategy enhanced chaotic evolution algorithm for global optimization and engineering applications

Mengnan Zhang; Longquan Yong; Yuanyuan Zhang

卷期
V19 · I5
门控
METADATA_CHANGED
DOI
10.1007/s12065-026-01239-7
证据层级
METADATA

官方论文页面link.springer.com · 2026-10-24 12:34 CST · 论文身份与出版状态证据 DOIdoi.org · 2026-10-24 12:34 CST · 规范化论文身份

摘要（可展开，供朗读/NotebookLM）

论文题名

A multi-strategy enhanced chaotic evolution algorithm for global optimization and engineering applications

期刊：Evolutionary Intelligence · DOI：10.1007/s12065-026-01239-7 · 语言：en · 摘要来源：出版方摘要

Chaotic Evolution Optimization (CEO) is a meta-heuristic optimization method inspired by the hyper-chaotic dynamics of two-dimensional discrete amnesia mapping. It enhances global exploration by simulating co-evolution among chaotic individuals and employing chaotic mapping to generate random search directions. Despite its competitiveness, the CEO algorithm has limitations in convergence accuracy, easy to fall into local optima, and premature convergence, particularly in complex problem domains. In this paper, a Multi-strategy Chaotic Evolution Optimization (MCEO) algorithm is proposed. It leverages four improvement strategies to mitigate these issues and synergistically boost the algorithm’s performance. First, a good point set initialization strategy generates a more uniform initial population for faster convergence into promising regions. Second, a novel nonlinear dynamic adjustment factor is designed to balance between global exploration and local exploitation, thereby improving convergence accuracy. Third, a spiral-guided random walk strategy replaces the original position update in the local exploitation phase, preventing search homogenization. Finally, a lens opposition-based learning strategy updates the current best solution after each iteration to reduce the risk of falling into local optima. This experimental work comprised two components. A parameter sensitivity analysis and ablation experiments for the MCEO algorithm were conducted on the IEEE CEC2020 benchmark. Subsequently, the algorithm’s performance in multidimensional optimization was assessed using the CEC2022 and CEC2017 test suites, benchmarking it against nine other peer algorithms. Results show that MCEO algorithm exhibits significantly better convergence accuracy and stability in most scenarios. Additionally, validation on three constrained engineering design problems and one wireless sensor network coverage problem confirms that MCEO algorithm outperforms the basic CEO and eight other algorithms in both optimization performance and practical applicability.

论文题名

A stochastic Levenberg-Marquardt artificial neural network framework for numerical analysis of fractional-order quit smoking dynamics

期刊：Evolutionary Intelligence · DOI：10.1007/s12065-026-01247-7 · 语言：en · 摘要来源：出版方摘要

The dynamics of smoking cessation demonstrate long-term memory effects, which integer-order models cannot properly capture. This research addresses this gap by developing a Stochastic Levenberg-Marquardt Backpropagation Artificial Neural Network (SLMB-ANN) framework for solving a fractional-order quit smoking model (FOQSM) with a harmonic mean uptake function. The developed model divides the complete population into four disease transmission groups which include potential smokers P(t), occasional smokers L(t), chain smokers S(t) and quit smokers Q(t). The SLMB-ANN is trained on reference solutions generated by the Adam’s method for fractional orders 𝛼 = 0.6 , 0.7 , 0.8 , 0.9 . A comprehensive validation using error histograms, regression analysis ( 𝑅 = 1 ) and mean squared error ( MSE ≈ 10 − 9 ) demonstrates that the proposed method achieves absolute errors between 10 − 5 and 10 − 7 . The results confirm that the SLMB-ANN framework provides a stable, accurate and computationally efficient tool for analyzing non-linear fractional-order epidemiological systems and public health modeling.
